Review in stages: first the10 blocked ledger/shared questions and source/construct prerequisites; next64existing revision/retype/split recommendations and8review-ready assertion scopes; then29identity-only decisions. Do not activate or implement any of these in this PR.
One retain-as-is and10V1-incomplete retain recommendations may be grouped only after human review. Research-needed/rejection ledgers can be grouped by rationale, but should not become synthetic scientific records.

Rows include ledger/shared-question decisions, not that many independent scientific claims. Existing revision proposals are included in their existing-edge row, not counted twice. Evidence is attached to each assertion, not an independent activation request.

## D. New causal candidates

### REL-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0001 — INF-F07

Repeated encounter with the same factual statement/headline can alter subsequently rated belief confidence in controlled adult online/laboratory tasks. Ordinary judgment tasks often show increases; extreme implausibility, explicit veracity cues and initial accuracy focus bound the claim. No universal monotonic sign, transfer to arbitrary content, calibrated truth or behavior is asserted. Extreme implausibility is not a universal exclusion: a later five-presentation/100-point-scale experiment found higher ratings while ratings remained below midpoint.

Type: RELATIONSHIP; endpoints/target: INF-041, PSY-003. Lifecycle: REVIEW_READY. Recommendation: REVIEW_READY. Priority: HIGH.

Evidence: MIXED / MODERATE / MODERATE. Supporting: Previously presented fake-news headlines received higher accuracy ratings; no generalized monotonic dose function established.

Null/contrary/insufficient: The source reports no repetition effect for entirely implausible statements. Positive bias-adjusted pooled repetition effect with substantial heterogeneity; initial task and veracity cues matter. Accuracy-focused initial processing attenuated the adverse repetition pattern reported in comparison tasks. Repeated highly implausible statements received higher truth ratings than new statements, still below the scale midpoint. Exploratory results showed about half of participants with no or reversed pattern.

Boundaries: Repeated encounter with the same factual statement/headline can alter subsequently rated belief confidence in controlled adult online/laboratory tasks. Ordinary judgment tasks often show increases; extreme implausibility, explicit veracity cues and initial accuracy focus bound the claim. No universal monotonic sign, transfer to arbitrary content, calibrated truth or behavior is asserted. Extreme implausibility is not a universal exclusion: a later five-presentation/100-point-scale experiment found higher ratings while ratings remained below midpoint.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Experimental exposure precedes truth judgment; converging synthesis retains heterogeneity and small-study limitations. No causal inference from familiarity ratings alone.

Sources: SRC-433, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0001, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0007, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0188.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0003, CF-PSY-LAYER-0042, CF-PSY-LAYER-0051.

[Family package](PSY-F01/GOVERNANCE_DECISION_PACKAGE.md)

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

## J. EffectAssertions

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0001 — PSY-F01

Same factual headline/claim repeated within a declared initial-task and test protocol; target is belief confidence in that claim, not its objective accuracy. Ordinary initial tasks can increase rated truth; accuracy-focused instructions, veracity cues and implausibility can attenuate or remove detected effects. No arbitrary dose-response. Extreme implausibility is not a universal exclusion: a later five-presentation/100-point-scale experiment found higher ratings while ratings remained below midpoint.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0001, PSY-003. Lifecycle: REVIEW_READY. Recommendation: REVIEW_READY. Priority: HIGH.

Evidence: MIXED / MODERATE / MODERATE. Supporting: Previously presented fake-news headlines received higher accuracy ratings; no generalized monotonic dose function established.

Null/contrary/insufficient: The source reports no repetition effect for entirely implausible statements. Positive bias-adjusted pooled repetition effect with substantial heterogeneity; initial task and veracity cues matter. Accuracy-focused initial processing attenuated the adverse repetition pattern reported in comparison tasks. Repeated highly implausible statements received higher truth ratings than new statements, still below the scale midpoint. Exploratory results showed about half of participants with no or reversed pattern.

Boundaries: Same factual headline/claim repeated within a declared initial-task and test protocol; target is belief confidence in that claim, not its objective accuracy. Ordinary initial tasks can increase rated truth; accuracy-focused instructions, veracity cues and implausibility can attenuate or remove detected effects. No arbitrary dose-response. Extreme implausibility is not a universal exclusion: a later five-presentation/100-point-scale experiment found higher ratings while ratings remained below midpoint.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Do not operationally spread misinformation. Scientific observation does not authorize deception, targeting, practitioner use or platform intervention.

Sources: SRC-433, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0001, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0007, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0188.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0003, CF-PSY-LAYER-0042, CF-PSY-LAYER-0051.

[Family package](PSY-F01/GOVERNANCE_DECISION_PACKAGE.md)

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0002 — PSY-F01

For previously exposed short false claims in the reported adult US MTurk protocol, explanatory refutation versus plain false-tag retraction reduced belief in the same false proposition at approximately one week. This comparison is not a universal effect of any correction or a one-day benefit.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0002, PSY-003. Lifecycle: REVIEW_READY. Recommendation: REVIEW_READY. Priority: HIGH.

Evidence: MIXED / MODERATE / MODERATE. Supporting: Refutation produced lower false-claim belief than plain retraction in the previously exposed conditions; baseline-adjusted change supported the contrast.

Null/contrary/insufficient: No detected refutation advantage for direct belief ratings at one day; inference findings are not substituted for this null contrast. Pooled average was not statistically distinguishable from zero; detail/topic/polarization varied with correction success.

Boundaries: For previously exposed short false claims in the reported adult US MTurk protocol, explanatory refutation versus plain false-tag retraction reduced belief in the same false proposition at approximately one week. This comparison is not a universal effect of any correction or a one-day benefit.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Require accurate correction and relevant sources; no guaranteed acceptance, confidence calibration, accuracy benefit for other claims or permission to manipulate audiences.

Sources: SRC-450, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0002.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0003, CF-PSY-LAYER-0042, CF-PSY-LAYER-0051.

[Family package](PSY-F01/GOVERNANCE_DECISION_PACKAGE.md)

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0003 — PSY-F02

In the specified healthy young-adult aversive-noise task, a response-contingent termination protocol versus its pseudo-yoked noncontingent comparator increased perceived control OVER THAT TASK. Not a universal effect of control availability, not specifically a validated measure of stressor controllability, not chronic agency or psychological safety.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0003, PSY-013. Lifecycle: REVIEW_READY. Recommendation: REVIEW_READY. Priority: HIGH.

Evidence: MIXED / LIMITED / MODERATE. Supporting: Contingent-response group rated task control higher; effect cannot isolate experienced success, instruction, timing and task contingency as independent components.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Task-control ratings differed in expected order, while many stress/transfer contrasts failed to separate the stress groups.

Boundaries: In the specified healthy young-adult aversive-noise task, a response-contingent termination protocol versus its pseudo-yoked noncontingent comparator increased perceived control OVER THAT TASK. Not a universal effect of control availability, not specifically a validated measure of stressor controllability, not chronic agency or psychological safety.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Aversive research exposure is not a recommended action; no permission to impose noise or deception. Consent, ethics, safety, legal authority and feasibility independently required.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0013.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0007, CF-PSY-LAYER-0017.

[Family package](PSY-F02/GOVERNANCE_DECISION_PACKAGE.md)

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0004 — PSY-F02

Research question whether the same contingent-control protocol alters expected ineffectiveness of one's actions (PSY-015). Current evidence measures feelings of helplessness, not the exact expectation; direction remains UNKNOWN.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0003, PSY-015. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: See scoped mixed/insufficient findings; no isolated supportive contrast asserted.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Lower reported feelings of helplessness under controllable protocol; exact alignment to PSY-015 expected futility remains insufficient. EC versus YC helplessness difference not statistically detected; both stress groups differed from no-stress control.

Boundaries: Research question whether the same contingent-control protocol alters expected ineffectiveness of one's actions (PSY-015). Current evidence measures feelings of helplessness, not the exact expectation; direction remains UNKNOWN.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Do not infer learned helplessness, depression, chronic harm or treatment benefit; no causal summation with EA-003 and no practitioner eligibility.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0013.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0007.

[Family package](PSY-F02/GOVERNANCE_DECISION_PACKAGE.md)

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0005 — PSY-F03

In the accessed young-adult virtual ball-game protocols, stopping passes after initial inclusion can increase immediate feelings of being ignored/excluded compared with continued inclusion. The claim is about perceived exclusion DURING THAT GAME, not chronic rejection, actual friendship loss, physiological stress or future behavior. Age-six non-detection is explicitly outside this proposed adult scope.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0004, PSY-024. Lifecycle: REVIEW_READY. Recommendation: REVIEW_READY. Priority: HIGH.

Evidence: SUPPORTS / MODERATE / MODERATE. Supporting: Exclusion condition reported higher ignored/excluded ratings than inclusion. FoMO and attachment-anxiety moderation terms were not detected; they were measured, not randomized. Adults reported greater feeling ignored in the ostracism condition. No inference to attention performance or whole need profile is imported.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Age-six felt-ignored difference not statistically detected. Older children and adults differed; the younger-age result limits transport, not a demonstrated zero or within-adult contradiction.

Boundaries: In the accessed young-adult virtual ball-game protocols, stopping passes after initial inclusion can increase immediate feelings of being ignored/excluded compared with continued inclusion. The claim is about perceived exclusion DURING THAT GAME, not chronic rejection, actual friendship loss, physiological stress or future behavior. Age-six non-detection is explicitly outside this proposed adult scope.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Exclusion can cause distress. Research identity grants no permission to exclude, deceive or manipulate people; informed consent, debriefing, ethics, safety, legal authority and applicability must be independently evaluated.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0020, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0021.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0014, CF-PSY-LAYER-0019.

[Family package](PSY-F03/GOVERNANCE_DECISION_PACKAGE.md)

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0006 — PSY-F03

Research question: comparative feedback about own, perceived-peer and measured-peer drinking changes the exact PSY-016 prevalence/frequency judgment. Accessed abstracts support a norm-measure research direction but do not resolve volume-versus-frequency dimension, comparator detail or exact direction. Do not claim universal reduction or calibrated correctness.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0005, PSY-016. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: See scoped mixed/insufficient findings; no isolated supportive contrast asserted.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Reported perceived-norm changes, but accessed abstract does not resolve exact dimension/direction for the PSY-016 frequency/prevalence construct. Consumption improved at three months in both feedback conditions; reported indirect associations included norms→later drinking and drinking→later norms. These do not independently establish either mediation route or a direct exact norm estimand. No detected improvement in downstream alcohol outcomes. This is not a null direct effect on PSY-016 and not an equivalence test.

Boundaries: Research question: comparative feedback about own, perceived-peer and measured-peer drinking changes the exact PSY-016 prevalence/frequency judgment. Accessed abstracts support a norm-measure research direction but do not resolve volume-versus-frequency dimension, comparator detail or exact direction. Do not claim universal reduction or calibrated correctness.

Risk if approved incorrectly: No advice to disclose private drinking data, no universal truth/accuracy claim for survey norms and no automatic reduction in harm. Reference group, consent, privacy, stigma, feasibility and ethics unresolved.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0019, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0024, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0027.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0012.

[Family package](PSY-F03/GOVERNANCE_DECISION_PACKAGE.md)

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0007 — PSY-F04

Providing two content-aligned, intermediate-difficulty homework options rather than assigning a yoked option can increase unit-end self-reported INTEREST/ENJOYMENT OF THAT HOMEWORK among grades 9–12 students in comparable short, teacher-supervised instructional units. No universal all-task, culture, age, trait or long-term effect.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0006, PSY-032. Lifecycle: REVIEW_READY. Recommendation: REVIEW_READY. Priority: HIGH.

Evidence: MIXED / MODERATE / MODERATE. Supporting: Students reported higher homework interest/enjoyment under choice. This is not proof that increased perceived autonomy independently mediated the effect.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Average motivational benefits coexist with heterogeneity and reported no-effect contexts; this review does not warrant universal option-choice efficacy. Perceived choice changed, but intrinsic motivation, internal locus and volition did not detectably differ. This constrains transfer beyond the homework scope. No statistically detected effects on effort, value or pressure/tension. Do not convert the interest finding into all-dimensional motivation improvement.

Boundaries: Providing two content-aligned, intermediate-difficulty homework options rather than assigning a yoked option can increase unit-end self-reported INTEREST/ENJOYMENT OF THAT HOMEWORK among grades 9–12 students in comparable short, teacher-supervised instructional units. No universal all-task, culture, age, trait or long-term effect.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Missing data, lower-achieving-student transfer, crossover expectations, task-equivalence and reporting bias. Not increased skill, need satisfaction, autonomous regulation, effort, value, reduced pressure, learning or behavioral success by implication. Selection of tasks can change experienced content; no fidelity guarantee.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0028, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0029, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0030.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0017.

[Family package](PSY-F04/GOVERNANCE_DECISION_PACKAGE.md)

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0008 — PSY-F05

Question: does specified cyclic sighing change current anxiety intensity beyond a matched attention/control condition in comparable screened adults? No universal decrease or clinical/trait benefit is asserted.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0007, PSY-042. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: See scoped mixed/insufficient findings; no isolated supportive contrast asserted.

Null/contrary/insufficient: State-anxiety reports fell after sessions in all four arms. This does not establish a specific cyclic-sighing advantage. No significant between-group difference in state-anxiety change; positive-affect superiority is a different outcome. No detected trait-anxiety changes or between-group differences.

Boundaries: Question: does specified cyclic sighing change current anxiety intensity beyond a matched attention/control condition in comparable screened adults? No universal decrease or clinical/trait benefit is asserted.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Pre/post improvement alone does not identify efficacy; pooled breathwork is not a single technique. Expectancy, adherence, comparator and STAI construct overlap limit inference. No personal medical recommendation.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0042.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0021, CF-PSY-LAYER-0055.

[Family package](PSY-F05/GOVERNANCE_DECISION_PACKAGE.md)

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0009 — PSY-F05

Question: can specified sadness-eliciting clips change current sadness relative to specified neutral clips in comparable French-speaking young adult viewers? Exact clip list and same-item contrast remain unresolved; no universal film effect.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0008, PSY-047. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: Review reports effective sadness and several other discrete-emotion inductions; happiness induction was not significant.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Film category altered emotional profiles; selected target emotions differentiated. Extracted target-versus-other-emotion tests are NOT the same-item sadness-versus-neutral effect required by the candidate. Average induction effectiveness varies by method, target emotion and sample; broad pooled evidence does not identify every discrete Driver effect.

Boundaries: Question: can specified sadness-eliciting clips change current sadness relative to specified neutral clips in comparable French-speaking young adult viewers? Exact clip list and same-item contrast remain unresolved; no universal film effect.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Target-versus-nontarget emotion differences within a clip category do not equal same-item sadness-versus-neutral contrasts. Mixed emotions, language, demand and sample composition limit inference. No mood/depression/comprehension effect.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0045, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0046, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0047.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0050.

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0010 — PSY-F06

Specified instructional visual cues in multimedia learning; evidence includes varied cue forms and learner populations. Exact occupied-resource measure and cue-specific contrast unresolved.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0009, PSY-057. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: See scoped mixed/insufficient findings; no isolated supportive contrast asserted.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Small average reduction in mixed subjective-load ratings, with heterogeneous learning results; exact resource occupancy is not separately isolated. Learning-relevant fixations and learning improved and load reduced in the reported synthesis; gaze measures do not establish a normalized attention proportion or available resources. Prior knowledge was not identified as an important moderator.

Boundaries: Specified instructional visual cues in multimedia learning; evidence includes varied cue forms and learner populations. Exact occupied-resource measure and cue-specific contrast unresolved.

Risk if approved incorrectly: No guaranteed construct change, benefit, legal permission or practitioner eligibility. Proxy and operation heterogeneity; no unmeasured downstream effect or double causal contribution.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0062, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0063.

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0011 — PSY-F06

N-back updating practice in the healthy-adult studies; exact variant, comparator, availability measurement and persistence require further extraction.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0010, PSY-058. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: Intermediate WM transfer improved immediately after training.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Transfer was substantially task-specific; other-domain/task effects were very small relative to n-back transfer. No convincing reliable far-transfer improvements versus treated controls; changes on WM measures did not explain far-transfer magnitude across studies.

Boundaries: N-back updating practice in the healthy-adult studies; exact variant, comparator, availability measurement and persistence require further extraction.

Risk if approved incorrectly: No guaranteed construct change, benefit, legal permission or practitioner eligibility. Proxy and operation heterogeneity; no unmeasured downstream effect or double causal contribution.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0059, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0060.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0021, CF-PSY-LAYER-0025, CF-PSY-LAYER-0030, CF-PSY-LAYER-0037, CF-PSY-LAYER-0041, CF-PSY-LAYER-0050.

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0012 — PSY-F07

After7min initial reading of specified short prose, one7min title-cued free-recall attempt without feedback versus7min restudy can increase probability of retrieving that prose's idea units under the same title cue at2days or1week among comparable18–24year-old undergraduates. Five-minute tests explicitly excluded from signed increase.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0011, PSY-066. Lifecycle: REVIEW_READY. Recommendation: REVIEW_READY. Priority: HIGH.

Evidence: MIXED / MODERATE / MODERATE. Supporting: Initial retrieval yielded higher delayed idea-unit recall than matched-time restudy.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Restudy produced higher immediate recall than retrieval practice. Repeated testing favored one-week recall, but restudy favored five-minute recall. No group difference in perceived readability was detected. Recall testing benefits retention more than recognition testing in the review; effort/episodic accounts remain alternatives.

Boundaries: After7min initial reading of specified short prose, one7min title-cued free-recall attempt without feedback versus7min restudy can increase probability of retrieving that prose's idea units under the same title cue at2days or1week among comparable18–24year-old undergraduates. Five-minute tests explicitly excluded from signed increase.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Counterbalanced controlled study not asserted randomized; narrow sample/materials/cue and scoring limits. Immediate5min reversal explicit. Exp2 unequal study/test durations not an identical dose contrast. No comprehension, calibration, confidence, readiness across all memories, trait change or practitioner permission.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0070, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0071.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0030.

[Family package](PSY-F07/GOVERNANCE_DECISION_PACKAGE.md)

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0013 — PSY-F07

Specified confirming post-identification feedback in video/photolineup experiments; exact current-versus-retrospective confidence estimand and comparator unresolved.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0012, PSY-068. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: See scoped mixed/insufficient findings; no isolated supportive contrast asserted.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Confirming feedback inflated certainty for correct/mistaken identifications and rejections; effect depended on rating format and latency. Feedback did not alter the confidence-accuracy correlation while producing overconfidence.

Boundaries: Specified confirming post-identification feedback in video/photolineup experiments; exact current-versus-retrospective confidence estimand and comparator unresolved.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Feedback may inflate overconfidence without accuracy; no benefit, lawful/ethical permission or recommendation. Adult pristine initial lineup evidence is not post-feedback validation.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0072.

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0014 — PSY-F08

Selected daily behaviors repeated at declared recurring cues; exact control-adjusted causal change in context-specific learned habit propensity not established by current source extraction.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0013, PSY-070. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: See scoped mixed/insufficient findings; no isolated supportive contrast asserted.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Automaticity increased on average with heterogeneous trajectories and model fits. No between-condition differences in automaticity or enactment were detected. Both groups increased automaticity/enactment; repetition predicted automaticity. Pooled habit/automaticity scores improved, but measurements, protocols and time estimates varied substantially. Overtraining did not increase inflexible habit expression in the reported attempts.

Boundaries: Selected daily behaviors repeated at declared recurring cues; exact control-adjusted causal change in context-specific learned habit propensity not established by current source extraction.

Risk if approved incorrectly: No whole automaticity or expectancy profile change, habit guarantee, universal time threshold, numerical weight or practitioner permission. Exact operation/comparator and measurement remain unresolved.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0083, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0084, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0085, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0086.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0032, CF-PSY-LAYER-0034.

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0017 — PSY-F09

Repeated specified GNG practice in healthy adults; exact protocol, independent inhibitory-control measurement and active comparator required. No whole-capacity or durable transfer assertion from heterogeneous task scores.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0016, PSY-081. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: See scoped mixed/insufficient findings; no isolated supportive contrast asserted.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Pooled cognitive scores favorable, highly heterogeneous; SST-alone contrast not significant. Review Table1 selects Enge passive-control contrast. Overall longer-term pooled difference not significant; GNG/SST-alone nondetection; combined subgroup favorable. No adaptive advantage over active control; passive-control latency advantage does not identify inhibition function gain. Small average health-behavior difference, task and measurement moderation; immediate GNG outcomes stronger. Small average training benefit attenuated by bias correction; weaker against active controls; active mechanism unresolved.

Boundaries: Repeated specified GNG practice in healthy adults; exact protocol, independent inhibitory-control measurement and active comparator required. No whole-capacity or durable transfer assertion from heterogeneous task scores.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Task transfer, comparator choice, stimulus-specific learning and fatigue. No inferred self-control resource, trait change or clinical efficacy.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0095, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0104, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0106, SRC186.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0037.

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0018 — PSY-F10

Positive personal future-episode cue displayed during specified hypothetical monetary intertemporal choices in studied adults; no uncued persistence or general trait claim. Exact canonical discount-rate/curve specification remains unresolved.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0017, PSY-089. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: Cue-present future thinking reduced local discounting relative to recent-event control; relevant event content matters.

Null/contrary/insufficient: No reduction detected without relevant event cues; delay-only/demand cues not sufficient. Future horizon did not expand; recent-event control shortened horizon, challenging mediation interpretation. Average reduced discounting; positive future content more favorable, neutral/negative contrast near zero; task/control moderators. Reduced discounting and cigarette-demand intensity; no elasticity/craving effect. Adjusted awareness did not eliminate discounting result.

Boundaries: Positive personal future-episode cue displayed during specified hypothetical monetary intertemporal choices in studied adults; no uncued persistence or general trait claim. Exact canonical discount-rate/curve specification remains unresolved.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Convenience samples, hypothetical rewards, cue demands, measurement-model dependence and no clinical/financial benefit inference.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0107, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0109, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0110.
Shared issues: CF-PSY-LAYER-0040.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: Positive average default-choice effect; substantial heterogeneity across domains and mechanisms.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Several included studies report no detected default effect. Two included studies show effects opposite the common default-uptake direction.

Boundaries: Specified preselected option versus active-choice/alternative-default comparator in declared choice domain; independent subjective preference measure needed.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Autonomy, coercion, accessibility and conflicts of interest; no automatic welfare or practitioner permission.

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0023 — PSY-F12

Specified high-control health-message scripts in US college flossing/drinking and Slovenian masking/activity contexts; no general coercion or actual mandate scope.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0022, PSY-108. Lifecycle: REVIEW_READY. Recommendation: REVIEW_READY. Priority: HIGH.

Evidence: MIXED / MODERATE / MODERATE. Supporting: Higher-control scripts increased measured freedom threat in both topics. Script wording also changes derogation/content; not isolated one-word or independently randomized latent state. Perceived freedom threat higher for high-control messages in both topics. Downstream attitudes/intention nondetection in high-trust COVID group despite reactance, not automatic advocacy effect.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Threatening language generally related to increased freedom threat/reactance; variation by study/sample and persuasion link not independent state-level causal identification. Social-value threat provided stronger predictive account than freedom threat in reported analyses; partial suppression complicates unique freedom attribution. Gain/loss contrasts did not detect systematic differences in these outcomes; high-control language findings differ. Composite reactance operationalization includes anger/negative cognition.

Boundaries: Specified high-control health-message scripts in US college flossing/drinking and Slovenian masking/activity contexts; no general coercion or actual mandate scope.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Specific populations, bundled manipulations and construct/proxy limits; no practitioner permission, behavioral benefit or model eligibility.

Sources: SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0136, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0137, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0138, SRC-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0156, SRC200.
Shared issues: None assigned..

PENDING — APPROVE / MODIFY / REJECT

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: Mostly250ms experience can make longer delays receive higher agency ratings; universal short-delay increase is contradicted by learning-context reversal.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Implicit effects replicated but between-person correlations between implicit and explicit measures, and between implicit measures, were not detected. Operational mismatch and pooling can mislead interpretation; within/between-person timing relations may differ.

Boundaries: Bounded keypress/feedback task with explicit learned delay distribution and0–250ms conditions.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Specific populations, bundled manipulations and construct/proxy limits; no practitioner permission, behavioral benefit or model eligibility.

### EA-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0030 — PSY-F14

Specified outgoing-behavior instructions versus contemporaneous sham over one week; do not combine with PEACH package as same operation.

Type: EFFECT_ASSERTION; endpoints/target: HT-CAND-PSY-LAYER-0029, PSY-122. Lifecycle: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Recommendation: RESEARCH_NEEDED. Priority: NORMAL.

Evidence: INSUFFICIENT / LIMITED / LOW. Supporting: See scoped mixed/insufficient findings; no isolated supportive contrast asserted.

Null/contrary/insufficient: Overall positive affect/authenticity benefits but more introverted participants reported less positive benefit and greater negative affect/tiredness/lower authenticity. Self changes aligned with goals; observer increases clearer than decreases; no continued untreated comparator at final follow-up. Extraversion self change concentrated in sociability, not allfacets; conscientiousness organization/productiveness notuniformresponsibility. Scalar invariance concerns. Observers generally showed small nonsignificant changes; not equivalence or proof of zero. Completion predicted growth; mere acceptance did not. Selection and actual behavior differ.

Boundaries: Specified outgoing-behavior instructions versus contemporaneous sham over one week; do not combine with PEACH package as same operation.

Risk if approved incorrectly: Component/package, time-scale, within/between-person, self-report and multidimensional-scope mismatch; no automatic therapeutic/practitioner permission.

### ARCH-PSY-LAYER-0001 — PSY-F01

How should feature/dimension-specific evidence for a multidimensional Driver be normalized without implying uniform whole-construct change?

Type: SHARED_ISSUE_NOT_SCIENTIFIC_RECORD; endpoints/target: PSY-002, PSY-022, PSY-065, PSY-071, PSY-073, PSY-097, PSY-113, PSY-120, PSY-121, PSY-122, PSY-123, PSY-124, PSY-125, PSY-126. Lifecycle: NON_GOVERNED_LEDGER. Recommendation: BLOCKED_NEEDS_GOVERNANCE_INPUT. Priority: HIGH.

Evidence: SEE_LINKED_FAMILY_REVIEW / NOT_ASSESSED / NOT_ASSESSED. Supporting: Existing INF-F03 feature-scope issue recurs for attitude certainty versus Attitude Strength. No whole-profile effect candidate retained; no AE04 expansion or production repair. PSY-F03 procedural fairness versus multidimensional Perceived Fairness presents the same unresolved feature-scope problem; no effect retained on the whole profile. PSY-F07 DomainKnowledge amount/organization raises the same feature-scope boundary; selected recall/concept-map score cannot imply whole-profile improvement. No production repair. F08 automaticity facets and reinforcement probability/value profiles also require exact normalized scope; no whole-profile effects retained. F11 social-identification profile includes centrality/solidarity/satisfaction/self-stereotyping; one component cannot imply uniform profile change. REL-PSY-065 remains retype-review only. F13 researcher expertise/trustworthiness and face trust ratings cannot automatically reduce/increase every credibility dimension. New whole-profile effect not retained; existing human-governed INF-F03 scoped claim remains unchanged. F14 same-PEACH-dataset facet reanalysis directly demonstrates that domain-score movement need not imply every facet changes; no whole-profile trait effect activated or architecture repaired.

Null/contrary/insufficient: See linked source/construct reviews.

Boundaries: How should feature/dimension-specific evidence for a multidimensional Driver be normalized without implying uniform whole-construct change?

Risk if approved incorrectly: Do not resolve by silent canonical edits or schema expansion.

Sources: See linked source/construct issue; no invented supporting study..
